- **ctf-forensics**: Investigate files, memory, logs, and digital artifacts. ([ruwwww/agent-skills](https://github.com/ruwwww/agent-skills/tree/HEAD/hermes-skills/security/ctf-forensics))
- **ctf-forensics-challenges**: Use when competing in a CTF forensics challenge, practicing in a lab, or analyzing evidence you are authorized to handle. Covers the forensics workflow, identifying file types and carving data, analyzing packet captures with Wireshark and tshark, triaging disk and memory images, extracting hidden data with steganography tools, reading metadata, and reassembling fragmented evidence into the flag. Use when a challenge hands you a pcap, disk image, memory dump, image, or mystery file, or when you need to reconstruct what happened from artifacts. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf/ctf-forensics-challenges))
- **ctf-forensics-challenges-disk-image-triage**: Use when a CTF forensics challenge provides a disk image, or when triaging storage media you are authorized to examine. Covers partition enumeration with mmls and fdisk, file listing with fls including deleted entries, file recovery with icat, carving unallocated space with blkls, building timelines with mactime, evidence preservation, hash verification, and checking slack space, alternate data streams, and shadow copies. Use when handed a .img, .dd, .E01, or .vmdk, when the flag is in a deleted file, or when you need to reconstruct file activity on the image. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf/ctf-forensics-challenges/sub/ctf-forensics-challenges-disk-image-triage))
- **ctf-forensics-challenges-pcap-analysis**: Use when a CTF forensics challenge provides a packet capture, or when analyzing network evidence you are authorized to handle. Covers capture triage with capinfos and protocol hierarchy, display filters for HTTP, DNS, FTP, ICMP and TLS metadata, following TCP and UDP streams, exporting transferred files, reassembling exfiltrated data with Python and scapy, and spotting C2 beacons and DNS tunneling. Use when handed a .pcap or .pcapng, when cleartext credentials or files crossed the wire, or when data was smuggled out over DNS, ICMP, or odd protocols. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf/ctf-forensics-challenges/sub/ctf-forensics-challenges-pcap-analysis))
- **ctf-forensics-challenges-steganography**: Use when a CTF forensics challenge hides data inside images, audio, video, or text files, or when analyzing media you are authorized to examine. Covers PNG LSB analysis with zsteg, steghide extraction and stegseek brute forcing, appended archive detection with binwalk, EXIF and metadata inspection, audio spectrogram analysis for hidden images, whitespace and unicode text steganography, and layered hiding where one method conceals another. Use when an image looks normal but must hide something, when audio sounds odd, or when file sizes do not match their visible content. ([mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal](https://github.com/mizazhaider-ceh/AgentArsenal/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf/ctf-forensics-challenges/sub/ctf-forensics-challenges-steganography))
- **ctf-forensics-disk**: Disk and memory forensics CTF workflow — partition analysis, filesystem reconstruction, deleted file recovery, file carving, memory dump analysis (Volatility), disk image mounting, LUKS encrypted volume recovery, APFS/ZFS snapshot analysis, Docker layer extraction, RAID reconstruction, VMware snapshot conversion, and MFT/USN journal parsing for authorized lab/competition material. ([salmanabdurrahman/pi-pentest-agent](https://github.com/salmanabdurrahman/pi-pentest-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf-forensics-disk))
- **ctf-forensics-extraction**: Digital forensics extraction pipelines for CTF challenges — binwalk, Volatility, PCAP, steganography, disk images, metadata. Lean skill with on-demand reference files. - **ctf-forensics-network**: Network forensics CTF workflow — PCAP analysis (Wireshark/tshark), protocol dissection, TLS decryption (SSLKEYLOGFILE, RSA key, session key derivation), HTTP object extraction, DNS covert channel detection, USB HID keyboard/mouse decode, TCP stream reconstruction, ICMP tunneling detection, SMB/NTLM credential extraction, VoIP/SIP call reconstruction, BCD encoding, timing-based covert channels, and wireless frame analysis for authorized lab/competition material. ([salmanabdurrahman/pi-pentest-agent](https://github.com/salmanabdurrahman/pi-pentest-agent/tree/HEAD/skills/ctf-forensics-network))

Do not use it when the vulnerability is already understood and the remaining task is exploitation; use pwn instead. Do not use it for pure web workflows, log or disk forensics, or standalone crypto problems unless reversing the implementation is the real blocker.
